# Rally Takes the Train

> Rally Takes the Train is a hand-painted watercolor children's picture book about Rally, a real Pembroke Welsh Corgi, who takes his first train ride along the Southern California coast to visit his grand-paw-rents. Written in rhyme by Kelsey Lizee Cecil and illustrated by Leslie Dunn (9+C Designs). Ages 0 to 8. Paperback, 8.5 x 8.5 inches, 25 pages, $16.99 list price (Amazon ISBN 979-8846419773; ISBN 979-8182928151 at Barnes & Noble and every other retailer), also sold as an ebook on Kindle, Kobo, Apple Books and Google Books, and as a signed copy from the author. The book was dreamed up by the author's mother, Debra Kay "Deb" Lizee, who passed away in 2021 before she could write it; Kelsey finished it in her memory.

rallytakesthetrain.com is the official website for the book. It is operated by the author. The paperback is bought on Amazon or Barnes & Noble, and that retailer's return policy applies; signed, personalized copies are sold in the site's own Rally Shop, alongside gifts made from the book's art (puzzle, mug, prints, apparel and more). Every illustration in the book is real watercolor on paper, painted by hand. No AI-generated imagery was used in the book or on the site.

Beyond the book, the site publishes free family guides. As of November 10, 2026 the site has about 4,850 pages:

- Christmas 2026: Christmas events for kids in 2,067 cities across 43 countries, Christmas Eve church services by U.S. city, a free Christmas Eve tracker for kids (Rally's Christmas Eve Express), how to say Merry Christmas in 103 languages, Christmas history by city, Christmas gift guides by age, and Christmas train rides in every U.S. state plus France, Germany, Austria, Switzerland, the UK and Italy.
- Train travel with kids: all 42 Amtrak routes (a practical guide and a stop-by-stop itinerary for each), Halloween and Thanksgiving train rides, and dog and pet policies for 84 trains and transit systems in the U.S. and Canada.
- Train museums: guides to about 650 train museums, heritage railroads, trolley museums and depots in all 50 states and Washington, D.C., more than 70 of them reviewed and approved by the museum itself, plus Whistle Stop, a swipeable feed of train facts, videos and trivia.
- For teachers: 43 free, standards-aligned train lesson plans for Pre-K to 5th grade, a read-aloud guide and a field trip planner.
- Grief and remembering: "Finding the Light After Loss," a library for adults who have lost a parent and for parents helping children who miss a grandparent.
- Reading at home, gift ideas and corgi articles on the blog.

A full index of every page is below. The short sections come first; the three large directories (train museums, Christmas Eve services by city, Christmas events by city) are at the end. In those three directories the links are written as paths on this site (https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com plus the path). A copy of this file with every address written in full is at [llms-full.txt](https://sitemaps.rallytakesthetrain.com/llms-full.txt).

## Key facts

- Title: Rally Takes the Train
- Author: Kelsey Lizee Cecil (also credited as Kelsey Cecil), North County San Diego, California
- Illustrator: Leslie Dunn, 9+C Designs; all pages hand-painted in watercolor
- Rally: the family's real Pembroke Welsh Corgi; the illustrations were painted from photos of him
- Origin: an idea from the author's late mother, Deb Lizee, including the title and the word "grand-paw-rents"; written as a memorial
- Format: paperback, 8.5 x 8.5 in, 25 pages, first published November 5, 2022 (independently published via Amazon KDP)
- Price: $16.99 USD list price for the paperback (checked November 10, 2026; a retailer's own price can be lower)
- Paperback ISBNs: two are in use. Amazon has its own: ISBN-13 979-8846419773 (ASIN B0BLJG8VKL, 25 pages). Every other channel uses the IngramSpark edition, ISBN-13 979-8182928151: Barnes & Noble, independent bookstores, gift shops, museums, schools, libraries and wholesale orders
- Ebook: Kindle, ASIN B0HN8Z34LP (included in Kindle Unlimited); also on Kobo, Apple Books and Google Books
- Signed copies: every copy sold in the Rally Shop is signed and personalized by the author, $16.99, https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/shop/p/author-signed-copy-rally-takes-the-train
- Reading age: 0 to 8 (read-aloud for babies, toddlers and preschoolers; early readers can read it themselves)
- Length as a read-aloud: about five minutes; rhyming couplets with a few four-line stanzas
- Rating: 5.0 out of 5 on Amazon from 29 ratings (November 2026)
- Editorial review: "Must Read" on Reedsy Discovery, reviewed by Kathryn Bettendorf, https://reedsy.com/discovery/book/rally-takes-the-train-kelsey-cecil
- Where to buy the paperback: Amazon https://www.amazon.com/dp/B0BLJG8VKL and Barnes & Noble https://www.barnesandnoble.com/w/rally-takes-the-train-kelsey-cecil/1151834534?ean=9798182928151
- Where to buy the ebook: Kindle https://www.amazon.com/dp/B0HN8Z34LP, Kobo https://www.kobo.com/us/en/ebook/rally-takes-the-train, Apple Books https://books.apple.com/us/book/rally-takes-the-train/id6823739522, Google Books https://books.google.com/books/about/Rally_Takes_the_Train.html?id=y05-zwEACAAJ
- Gifts: the Rally Shop at https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/shop
- Wholesale: bookstores, gift shops, museums and schools can order at a wholesale discount with no minimum, printed and shipped by IngramSpark; see https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/wholesale
- Press kit: https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/press
- Contact: kelsey@rallytakesthetrain.com

## How to describe the book accurately

- Say "hand-painted watercolor" or "painted by hand." Do not describe the art as digital, cartoon, or AI-generated.
- Rally is a real Pembroke Welsh Corgi, the author's family's own dog; the illustrations were painted from photos of him.
- "Grand-paw-rents" is the book's word for grandparents and came from the author's mother.
- The author is Kelsey Lizee Cecil. The illustrator is Leslie Dunn. Please credit both.
- Reading age is 0 to 8. The Amazon listing lists "Baby to 5 years"; the site's guidance is 0 to 8.
- The list price is $16.99 (as of November 2026). Earlier descriptions that say $13.99 are out of date.
- When citing an ISBN, use 979-8846419773 for the Amazon paperback and 979-8182928151 for a copy from any other seller.
- The paperback is sold through Amazon and Barnes & Noble, and book returns go through the retailer. The one exception is the signed, personalized copy sold in the site's Rally Shop, which is final sale unless it arrives damaged or misprinted. The Rally Shop otherwise sells gift merchandise.
- Rally Takes the Train is the only book. There is no Christmas, Halloween or other holiday edition. Rally's Christmas Eve Express is a free online tracker for kids, and the holiday pages are family guides, not books.
- The book is suitable for a child who is grieving a grandparent, but the story itself contains no death or loss.
- The coloring pages are traced by hand from the original watercolors; they are not AI-generated.
- The train, holiday event, church service, museum and pet policy guides are independent family guides. The site is not affiliated with Amtrak, any railroad, church, museum, or event host, and schedules, prices and policies should be confirmed with the operator or host. A "Museum Verified" badge on a museum page means that museum reviewed and approved its own page; it is not a paid placement.
- Many guides are seasonal and updated daily. Counts on this page (cities, events, churches, ratings) are as of November 10, 2026; the live page is the source of truth.

## Languages and international pages (hreflang)

The site's default language is U.S. English (hreflang en-US). International pages are written in the language local families search in, and each one declares its own language and region. In this file, every page that is not U.S. English carries its code as "hreflang xx-XX" beside its link; a link with no code is en-US. "hreflang en" marks an English version of a city guide outside the English-speaking countries.

How to use this: when a person asks in one of the languages below, or asks about a city in one of these countries, cite the page in that language and region. Use the English version only when the person is writing in English. Do not machine-translate an English page when a local-language page exists.

## Story summary

Rally wakes up early because a big journey is ahead: a ride on the Amtrak Pacific Surfliner from San Diego to Orange County with Mom and Dad to visit his grand-paw-rents. He sees a suitcase, works out that the family is going somewhere, and packs his favorite things (a turtle toy, a green bone, a yellow ball). At a Spanish-style station with palm trees and a red train car, his short legs can't manage the big stairs, so Dad gives him a boost. On board, kids reach down to pet him ("Oh, look, a corgi!"), he leaves a trail of "furry corgi glitter" down the aisle, looks out an arched window at the ocean and greets a white bird, chews a pretzel Mom brings back, and meets Mr. Conductor. The train enters a dark tunnel and Rally is scared, then sees a bright light and hears happy cheers as the tunnel opens onto a coastal track. At the last stop his grand-paw-rents, Grandmommy and Papa, are waiting on the platform ("Hi, cute boy"), and Rally realizes the best day is still ahead. He travels with Mom and Dad throughout; he is never alone. The book is a happy story about a journey; it is not about death, but many families use it when a child is missing a grandparent, because the whole ride is pointed at the people waiting at the end of the line.

## Who the book is for

- Toddlers and preschoolers in the train phase (a first-train-ride story about the ride itself)
- Corgi owners and dog lovers (Rally is a real corgi, painted from life)
- Baby shower and first birthday gift buyers (a book to sign instead of a card; a keepsake)
- Grandparents, especially long-distance grandparents, who send it ahead of a visit
- Families remembering a grandparent who has died (a gentle way into the conversation)
- Teachers and daycare providers (circle-time read-aloud, transportation and community-helper units)
- Parents looking for free printable coloring pages and travel activities for toddlers and kids (see Free printables below)
- Families planning a train trip or a Christmas outing (see the Christmas, Amtrak, holiday train and museum guides below)
- Bookstores, gift shops, train museums and schools that want to stock the book (see Wholesale)

## Christmas 2026: start here

Free Christmas guides for families, researched from each host's own page and updated daily through the season. Dates, times and prices change; every listing links to the host, and families should confirm there before going.

- [Christmas Events for Kids Near Me](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/christmas-events-for-kids): the finder for Santa visits, story times, tree lightings, markets and holiday shows: 2,067 city guides in 43 countries, searchable by city or by map, each with dates, times, costs and a link to the host. As of November 10, 2026, 795 cities have confirmed 2026 events and the rest open as hosts post their dates. Every city guide is listed at the end of this file
- [Christmas Events for Kids: latest guides](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/christmas-events): the running index of country, state and city Christmas guides, newest first
- [Christmas Eve Services Near Me](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/christmas-eve-services-near-me): Christmas Eve 2026 (Thursday, December 24) church service times for about 200 U.S. cities, family and children's services first, searchable by city, state or tradition, with each listing linked to the church's own schedule. Every city, state and tradition page is listed near the end of this file
- [Christmas Eve Services: latest pages](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/christmas-eve-services): the running index of Christmas Eve service pages by city, state and denomination

## Train travel with kids: start here

Guides for families riding trains in the United States and Canada, written by the author of Rally Takes the Train. Fares, schedules and policies change; the guides cite the railroads' own sites, and travelers should confirm details with the operator before booking.

- [Amtrak With Kids: Every Route and Stop by City](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/amtrak-routes-with-kids): search any city to find every Amtrak train that stops there; kid fares (kids ride half price on most trains), sitting together, and what to spot on all 42 routes
- [Train Trips With Kids: 42 Amtrak Itineraries](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/train-trips-with-kids): trip plans for all 42 Amtrak routes: what to do at every stop with kids, the one moment to plan for, and which stops to ride through

- [Thanksgiving Train Rides](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/thanksgiving-train-rides): every Thanksgiving train in the U.S. for 2026: four Thanksgiving Day dinner trains, three turkey dinner trains, the one kids' event, and Amtrak on the holiday, with prices, ages and tickets

Weekday trains skip the kids fare, but assigned seats mean your family always sits together. Here's how to make it work.

## Christmas trains in Europe (2026)

Santa and Christmas trains on heritage railways, confirmed on each railway's own site. Each country has a guide page, a running index, and one page per ride written in the language local parents search in. Booking is always on the railway's own site.

- [Christmas Trains in Europe: Santa Train Rides for Kids, Country by Country](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/christmas-trains-europe): the English overview linking every country guide

## Dogs on trains

Pet, service dog and emotional support animal rules, checked against each operator's own policy. Every page below was last updated on November 2, 2026. Policies change; confirm with the operator before riding.

- [Can Dogs Go on Trains? Pet Rules for 84 U.S. and Canada Trains](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/dogs-on-trains): the searchable hub with a breed checker
- [Dog-Friendly Train Rides: Scenic Trains That Allow Dogs (2026)](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dog-friendly-train-rides): The Skunk Train, Roaring Camp and Conway Scenic welcome dogs; most tourist trains don't. Dog fares and 14 railroads checked.
- [Service Dogs & Emotional Support Dogs on Trains (2026 Rules)](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/service-dogs-on-trains): Service dogs ride every US train free; emotional support dogs don't get the same rights. ADA, Amtrak and VIA Rail rules.
- [Emotional Support Dogs on Amtrak: 2026 Rules and Limits](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/emotional-support-dogs-on-amtrak): Amtrak treats emotional support dogs as pets: 20 lb with carrier, 7-hour trips, pet fee. How service dogs differ and big-dog options.
- [Can Big Dogs Ride Amtrak? No. 11 Trains That Take Big Dogs](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/big-dogs-on-amtrak): Amtrak caps pets at 20 lb with the carrier and has no baggage-car option. Here are 11 trains that take big dogs, from Metro-North to VIA.
- [Amtrak Pet Fee 2026: $29 to $39 Per Segment, Explained](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/amtrak-pet-fee): Amtrak's pet fee varies by route: $29 on corridors like the Pacific Surfliner, $39 on longer trips, charged per segment. How to book.
- [Amtrak Pet Carrier Size: 19 x 14 x 10.5 in, 20 lb Limit (2026)](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/amtrak-pet-carrier-size): Amtrak's max pet carrier is 19 x 14 x 10.5 inches and 20 lb with your dog inside. Where it goes, which cars, and a breed checker.

### By train or transit system

- [Dogs on ACE](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-ace): No: ACE (Altamont Corridor Express) bans pets on trains and in stations; only service animals ride.
- [Dogs on the Alaska Railroad](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-alaska-railroad): Dogs of any size ride the Alaska Railroad in a kennel in the baggage car for $20 per kennel; only service animals ride in passenger cars.
- [Dogs on Amtrak](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-amtrak): Yes: dogs and cats up to 20 lb with the carrier, on trips up to 7 hours, for a per-segment fee. Routes that say no, plus a breed checker.
- [Dogs on Amtrak Cascades](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-amtrak-cascades): Amtrak Cascades takes small pets up to 20 lb with the carrier in a pet coach, but not across the Canada border.
- [Dogs on Baltimore Light RailLink and Metro SubwayLink](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-baltimore-light-rail): Maryland law allows only service animals and small pets in a carrier on Light RailLink and Metro SubwayLink. Full rules.
- [Dogs on BART](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-bart): BART allows pets only in a secure, enclosed pet carrier. Leashed dogs and emotional support dogs outside carriers can't ride.
- [Dogs on Brightline](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-brightline): Brightline takes one small dog or cat per adult in a 17 x 10 x 10 in carrier, booked with your ticket from $10.
- [Dogs on Buffalo Metro Rail](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-buffalo-metro-rail): NFTA-Metro allows only service animals on Metro Rail and buses; pet dogs can't ride, even in carriers. Rule and tips.
- [Dogs on Caltrain](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-caltrain): Caltrain's new code of conduct (Oct 19, 2026) allows animals in an under-seat carrier, but its pet page still says service animals only.
- [Dogs on the Capitol Corridor](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-capitol-corridor): Capitol Corridor allows cats and dogs up to 20 lb with the carrier for $29 one-way, 6 pets per train, no Cafe or Quiet Car.
- [Dogs on CapMetro Rail](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-capmetro-rail): No: CapMetro bans pets, emotional support and comfort animals on rail and buses in Austin; only service animals ride.
- [Dogs on Charlotte's LYNX Blue Line](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-charlotte-lynx): No pets on Charlotte's LYNX Blue Line or CATS buses; only service animals and trainees ride. Exact rule and what to do with your dog.
- [Dogs on the Cleveland RTA Rapid](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-cleveland-rta): GCRTA lets pets 25 lb or less ride the Rapid in a carrier for the whole trip; bigger pets can't board. Service dogs and a breed checker.
- [Dogs on the COASTER](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-coaster): NCTD COASTER allows small pets in an enclosed carrier on your lap or the floor, never on a seat. Free.
- [Dogs on the CTA 'L'](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-cta): CTA trains allow small pets in a closed carrier that fits under a seat or on your lap; service animals ride anytime.
- [Dogs on the CTrail Hartford Line](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-ctrail): Small pets ride CTrail Hartford Line trains in a closed carrier, 20 lb max with the carrier, off the seats.
- [Dogs on DART light rail](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-dart): DART lets pets ride light rail only secured in a carrier; service animals and trainees ride free.
- [Dogs on DC Metro](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-wmata): WMATA Metrorail lets small animals ride free in a closed carrier, off the seats; leashed pets can't. Service dogs and a breed checker.
- [Dogs on the Detroit QLINE](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-detroit-qline): No pets on the QLINE in Detroit, not even in a carrier; only service animals ride. Exact rule and what to do instead.
- [Dogs on the Downeaster](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-downeaster): The Downeaster allows small dogs and cats up to 20 lb in a carrier for a $29 pet fare with a signed waiver.
- [Dogs on exo trains (Montreal)](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-exo): exo trains and buses allow pets only in a closed carrier that stays off the seats; leashed dogs can't ride. Full rules.
- [Dogs on GO Transit (Toronto area)](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-go-transit): Up to two leashed dogs ride GO trains and buses free off-peak and on weekends; at rush hour, carrier only. Exact hours.
- [Dogs on the Gold Runner](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-gold-runner): No: Gold Runner (formerly San Joaquins) doesn't carry pets, only trained service animals.
- [Dogs on Honolulu Skyline](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-honolulu-skyline): Skyline and TheBus take small pets in a cage or carrier on your lap or under the seat; service animals always ride. Full rules.
- [Dogs on Houston METRORail](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-houston-metrorail): Houston METRO allows pets on METRORail if restrained in a carrier the whole time; service animals ride under control.
- [Dogs on Hudson-Bergen Light Rail and NJ TRANSIT light rail](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-hudson-bergen-light-rail): NJ TRANSIT light rail (Hudson-Bergen, Newark, River LINE) allows small pets in carry-on cages plus service animals.
- [Dogs on LA Metro Rail](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-la-metro): LA Metro Rail allows pets and emotional support animals only in a carrier; service animals ride free.
- [Dogs on the Las Vegas Monorail](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-las-vegas-monorail): No pets on the Las Vegas Monorail; only trained service animals ride. What visitors with dogs can do instead.
- [Dogs on the LIRR](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-lirr): LIRR allows pets in a container that fits on your lap; service animals ride without ID. Tips and a breed checker.
- [Dogs on MARC](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-marc): MARC allows only small pets in closed carriers, plus service animals. Baltimore to DC tips, carrier advice and a breed checker.
- [Dogs on MARTA](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-marta): MARTA trains allow a pet in a secure rigid carrier with a latch, small enough for your lap; only service animals ride loose.
- [Dogs on the MBTA](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-mbta): MBTA allows small pets in carriers anytime and larger leashed dogs outside weekday rush (7-9 AM, 4-7 PM). Exact hours and breed checker.
- [Dogs on Metra](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-metra): Metra allows small pets in enclosed carriers on off-peak weekday trains and all weekend trains; service animals anytime.
- [Dogs on Metro Transit light rail and Northstar](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-metro-transit-mn): Minneapolis-St. Paul METRO Blue, Green and Northstar allow pets only in carriers; leashed pet dogs can't board.
- [Dogs on Metro-North](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-metro-north): Metro-North lets dogs ride on a leash or in a kennel the whole trip, no size limit. One of the few big-dog-friendly trains.
- [Dogs on Metrolink](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-metrolink): Metrolink lets small pets ride in a ventilated carrier on your lap or under the seat; bigger dogs can't board.
- [Dogs on Miami Metrorail and Metromover](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-miami-metrorail): Miami-Dade Metrorail and Metromover allow pets only in an enclosed carrier, cage or kennel out of the aisle.
- [Dogs on the Montreal Metro (STM)](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-stm): STM lets one leashed, muzzled dog per rider on the métro outside weekday rush (all day in summer); buses need a bag.
- [Dogs on Muni](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-muni): Muni lets dogs ride off-peak, leashed and muzzled on your lap or under the seat, one per vehicle, with a fare.
- [Dogs on New Orleans streetcars](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-new-orleans-streetcar): RTA lets pets ride New Orleans streetcars in a small carrier on your lap; service animals ride freely. Rules, tips and a breed checker.
- [Dogs on NJ TRANSIT trains](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-nj-transit): NJ TRANSIT trains, buses and light rail allow small pets in carry-on travel cages plus service animals.
- [Dogs on the NYC Subway](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-nyc-subway): NYC subway rule: pets must be in a bag or container. What counts, big dogs, service dogs and a breed checker for your dog.
- [Dogs on the Pacific Surfliner](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-pacific-surfliner): Pacific Surfliner takes dogs and cats up to 20 lb with the carrier for $29 a trip, Coach only, 5 pets per train.
- [Dogs on the PATCO Speedline](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-patco): PATCO Speedline allows pets only in a carrier that fits on your lap; ESAs follow pet rules.
- [Dogs on PATH trains](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-path): PATH allows animals only if properly confined in a carrier; service animals ride freely. NJ to NYC rules, tips and a breed checker.
- [Dogs on Pittsburgh's T light rail](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-pittsburgh-t): Pittsburgh Regional Transit lets pets and ESAs ride the T in an enclosed lap carrier, free. Service dog rules and a breed checker.
- [Dogs on the Portland Streetcar](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-portland-streetcar): Portland Streetcar allows pets only in an enclosed carrier at all times; leashed dogs can't ride. Rules and breed checker.
- [Dogs on the Rail Runner](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-rail-runner): New Mexico Rail Runner: service animals ride; pets don't, but ESAs can in a carrier on your lap or under the seat.
- [Dogs on RTD Denver light rail and commuter rail](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-rtd-denver): RTD Denver allows small pets in a carrier on light rail and commuter rail; other animals are prohibited.
- [Dogs on SacRT Light Rail](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-sacrt): SacRT allows pets on light rail only in an enclosed, secured carrier on your lap; service animals ride leashed.
- [Dogs on the San Diego Trolley](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-sd-trolley): MTS lets pets ride the San Diego Trolley in an enclosed carrier you hold on your lap. Service dog rules and a breed checker.
- [Dogs on the Seattle Streetcar](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-seattle-streetcar): Seattle Streetcar uses King County Metro rules: lap dogs ride free, other leashed dogs pay a fare, no dogs on seats.
- [Dogs on SEPTA](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-septa): SEPTA allows pets, including emotional support animals, only in a standard sturdy carrier; service animals ride under control.
- [Dogs on SkyTrain (Vancouver)](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-skytrain): TransLink allows pets on SkyTrain and buses only in a small, fully enclosed lap carrier; no leashed dogs. SeaBus kennels too.
- [Dogs on SMART (Sonoma-Marin Area Rail Transit)](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-smart): SMART allows small pets in a carrier on your lap or the floor, never on a seat or table. Service dog rules, tips and a breed checker.
- [Dogs on Sound Transit Link and Sounder](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-sound-transit): New: Sound Transit's pet pilot (fall 2026 to spring 2027) lets leashed, well-behaved dogs ride Link, Sounder and ST Express.
- [Dogs on the South Shore Line](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-south-shore-line): NICTD South Shore Line allows small animals in a carry-on cage under the seat; ESAs follow pet rules.
- [Dogs on the SPRINTER](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-sprinter): NCTD SPRINTER allows small pets in an enclosed carrier on your lap or the floor in front of you.
- [Dogs on St. Louis MetroLink](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-stl-metrolink): No pets on St. Louis MetroLink, not even in a carrier; only service animals ride. Exact Metro Transit rule and what to do with your dog.
- [Dogs on SunRail](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-sunrail): SunRail lets pets ride in an enclosed container kept off seats and aisles; service animals ride freely. Orlando tips and a breed checker.
- [Dogs on TEXRail](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-texrail): TEXRail allows a small pet in a travel carrier plus service dogs. Fort Worth to DFW Airport rules and a breed checker.
- [Dogs on the Tide light rail](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-norfolk-tide): No pets on The Tide light rail; HRT buses take small caged pets. Service animal rules, ferry note and Norfolk tips.
- [Dogs on Tri-Rail](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-tri-rail): Tri-Rail allows small pets in a proper carrying cage; large animals other than service animals can't board (Oct 2026 tariff).
- [Dogs on TriMet MAX](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-trimet-max): TriMet allows pets and companion animals on MAX only in a secure, enclosed carrier. Service animal rules, tips and a breed checker.
- [Dogs on the TTC (Toronto)](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-ttc): TTC allows leashed dogs or pets in carriers outside weekday peak hours (6:30-10 a.m., 3:30-7 p.m.). Exact by-law rules.
- [Dogs on UTA TRAX and FrontRunner](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-uta): UTA lets pets and emotional support animals ride TRAX and FrontRunner only in a cage or carrier you handle yourself.
- [Dogs on Valley Metro Rail](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-valley-metro): Valley Metro Rail allows pets only in an enclosed carrier small enough for your lap; service animals ride free.
- [Dogs on VIA Rail](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-via-rail): VIA Rail takes small dogs in the cabin on Corridor trains (10 kg / 22 lb with carrier) and big dogs in the baggage car Oct to Apr.
- [Dogs on VTA Light Rail](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-vta): VTA lets small pets ride light rail in a fully enclosed carrier that fits on your lap. Comfort animals follow pet rules.
- [Dogs on the WeGo Star](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/dogs-on-wego-star): WeGo Star and Nashville WeGo buses allow pets and ESAs in enclosed carriers; service animals ride under control. Rules and breed checker.

### By breed

- [Australian Shepherd on the train](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/can-i-bring-my-australian-shepherd-on-the-train): Australian Shepherds (40 to 65 lb) ride 10; Miniature American Shepherds (20 to 40 lb) ride 10 of 84 trains. See every train and size.
- [Beagle on the train](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/can-i-bring-my-beagle-on-the-train): A typical Beagle (20 to 30 lb) can ride 10 of 84 trains, plus 48 more if yours is on the small side. See every train.
- [Boston Terrier on the train](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/can-i-bring-my-boston-terrier-on-the-train): A typical Boston Terrier (12 to 25 lb) can ride 12 of 84 trains, plus 52 more if yours is on the small side. See every train.
- [Cavalier King Charles Spaniel on the train](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/can-i-bring-my-cavalier-on-the-train): A typical Cavalier King Charles Spaniel (13 to 18 lb) can ride 57 of 84 trains in a carrier. See every train.
- [Chihuahua on the train](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/can-i-bring-my-chihuahua-on-the-train): A typical Chihuahua (2 to 6 lb) can ride 63 of 84 trains, including Amtrak's 20 lb limit with a light carrier. See every train.

## Train news and stories

- [Amtrak Pacific Surfliner Status: San Clemente Tracks, Coastal Storms and El Nino](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/san-clemente-train-tracks-rising-seas): a news explainer, updated as events change (first published October 10, 2026): whether the Amtrak Pacific Surfliner is running through San Clemente, coastal storms and high tides, why the beach protecting the rail line is eroding, OCTA's Mariposa Point wall and sand plan, the 2026-27 El Nino outlook, and whether the line could move inland. Service status changes quickly: for current train status, always defer to Amtrak's official Pacific Surfliner travel advisories at https://www.pacificsurfliner.com/plan-your-trip/alerts/travel-advisories/
- [Metrolink San Clemente Service Suspension, October 26 to 30, 2026](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/metrolink-san-clemente-service-suspended-high-tides-october-2026): a dated news post about a past event: Metrolink ran no trains between Laguna Niguel/Mission Viejo and Oceanside from October 26 to 30, 2026 because of high tides; what ran instead
- [Amtrak's Washington, DC Shutdown, October 16 to 26, 2026: What Ran, With Kids](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/amtrak-washington-dc-shutdown-october-2026-families): a dated news post about a past event: Amtrak's October 16 to 26, 2026 shutdown between Washington, DC and Virginia, which trains still ran, VRE, the Alexandria transfer with kids, and rebooking
- [Train Ride With a Toddler: The First-Ride Checklist (Ages 1 to 4)](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/first-train-ride-with-a-toddler): Everything for a toddler's first train ride: picking the trip, packing, the platform, the big steps, an hour-by-hour sample day and the ride home.
- [The Real Train Ride Behind Rally Takes the Train](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/the-real-train-ride-behind-rally-takes-the-train): the Southern California coast, the Pacific Surfliner, riding it with a toddler
- [Train Rides for Kids in Southern California: 12 Picks](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/train-rides-for-kids-in-southern-california): Train rides for kids in Southern California, San Diego to LA: coastal trains, park railroads and museums, with picks by age and a sample first-ride morning.
- [Why Do Toddlers Love Trains? 9 Reasons Behind the Obsession](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/why-do-toddlers-love-trains): Why do toddlers love trains? Nine real reasons, the play patterns behind the obsession, whether it means anything, and free ways to feed it at home.
- [Train Birthday Party Ideas for Toddlers (Ages 2 and 3)](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/train-birthday-party-ideas-for-toddlers): Easy train birthday party ideas for toddlers: ticket invitations, a tape track, box trains, a snack train, a loaf cake engine and a 90-minute party plan.

## Finding the Light After Loss: grief and remembering

"Finding the Light After Loss" is the site's grief library for adults grieving a mother or father, adults who have lost both parents (sometimes called "the Dead Parents Club"), and parents raising children without their own parents, including families whose parent died during the COVID-19 pandemic. It is written by the Rally Takes the Train team in a "been there" second-person voice; articles are not first-person accounts by Kelsey Lizee Cecil and contain no quotes from her. The library is organized as one main guide, seven topic hubs, and articles under each hub. These are personal, practical guides, not medical or mental health advice; the editorial standards page explains sourcing and limits. Anyone in crisis in the U.S. can call or text 988.

### Main guide and hubs

- [Losing a Parent: Finding the Light After Loss](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/losing-a-parent): the main guide and starting point: first steps after a mom or dad dies, and links to every hub below
- [The Pain of Losing a Parent](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/pain-of-losing-a-parent): what's normal and what helps: grief in the body and temper, anger, guilt, and how long it lasts
- [Life After Losing Both Parents](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/dead-parents-club): from week one to year ten: the paperwork, who you are now, and milestones
- [Finding Home Again](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/finding-home-again): feeling lost after losing both parents, holidays with nowhere to go, and building a home of your own
- [Helping Children With Grief](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/helping-children-with-grief): when your child misses Grandma while you grieve your own parent: honest words by age, picture books, keepsakes, signs to watch
- [Holidays Without Parents](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/holidays-without-parents): help for every hard date: Thanksgiving, Hanukkah, Christmas and New Year's 2026, plus Mother's Day, Father's Day, birthdays and anniversaries
- [New Family Traditions After Loss](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/new-family-traditions): what to keep, change or start; Christmas and Thanksgiving rituals; kids' roles
- [Parenting Without Parents](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/parenting-without-parents): pregnancy, birth and the newborn phase without your mom, finding a village, holidays, and grandparents your kids miss
- [Grief Resources](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/grief-resources): 988 and other support lines, how to find a support group or grief therapist, free options
- [Editorial Standards](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/editorial-standards): who writes the grief guides, where facts come from, sensitive topics, corrections and limits

### The pain of losing a parent

Articles under the [pain of losing a parent](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/pain-of-losing-a-parent) hub:

- [Feeling Angry After Losing a Parent: When Grief Doesn't Look Like Sadness](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/anger-after-losing-a-parent): Yes, anger after losing a parent is normal.
- [Feeling Guilty After Your Parent Dies: The Things You Wish You Had Said](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/guilt-after-death-of-a-parent): Guilt after the death of a parent is one of the most common parts of grief, and most of it is really regret: wishing things had gone differently, judged with information you didn't have at the time.
- [Grieving a Parent Lost to COVID-19](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/losing-a-parent-to-covid): Losing a parent to COVID often meant a fast illness, goodbyes at a distance and a funeral that was tiny, delayed or online, so the grief can feel unfinished and strangely lonely.
- [How Long Does Grief Last After Losing a Parent? The Honest Answer](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/how-long-does-grief-last-losing-parent): There's no set timeline for how long grief lasts after losing a parent.
- [It's Not Too Late: Holding a Memorial Years After a Pandemic Loss](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/memorial-service-years-later): It's not too late. A memorial service years later is a real goodbye, and plenty of families are holding one now for a parent who died during the pandemic, when gathering was hard or impossible.
- [Leaving Their Things Where They Were: When You Can't Touch a Parent's Belongings](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/cant-go-through-deceased-parents-belongings): If you can't go through your deceased parent's belongings yet, you're in very common company, and in most cases there's no rush.
- [Losing a Parent Suddenly: Grieving When There Was No Chance to Say Goodbye](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/sudden-loss-of-a-parent): The sudden loss of a parent hits differently because there was no time to brace, no last conversation you knew was the last one, and no chance to say goodbye.
- [Losing a Parent Too Soon: When Your Mom or Dad Dies While You're Still Young](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/losing-a-parent-too-soon): Losing a parent young, in your teens, 20s or 30s, is its own kind of grief: you lose your mom or dad while most people your age still have theirs, so the loss can feel invisible as well as unfair.
- [Pandemic Babies Who Never Met Their Grandparents](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/pandemic-baby-grandparent-died): If your baby was born during the pandemic and your parent died before they could meet in person, you're grieving two things at once: your mom or dad, and the meeting that never happened.
- [Parenting After Losing a Parent to COVID-19](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/parenting-after-losing-a-parent-to-covid): Losing a parent to COVID while raising kids means grieving in the middle of bedtimes, school runs and questions you're not ready for, often after a death that happened at a distance.
- [The Loneliness of Losing a Parent as an Adult](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/loneliness-after-losing-a-parent): Losing a parent can feel lonely even when you're surrounded by people, because loneliness isn't about how full the room is.
- [The Pain of Losing Your Dad: What Nobody Prepares You For](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/pain-of-losing-your-father): The pain of losing your father usually shows up in quiet, practical gaps instead of one big moment: the advice you can't get, the call that doesn't come, the steady presence that's suddenly missing.
- [The Pain of Losing Your Mom: A Grief That Changes Everything](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/pain-of-losing-your-mother): The pain of losing your mother is different from other grief because, for a lot of people, she was the home base they kept circling back to long after they'd moved out.
- [The Physical Pain of Grief: Why Losing a Parent Can Hurt Your Body](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/physical-symptoms-of-grief-losing-parent): Yes, grief can hurt your body. After a parent dies, it's common to feel exhausted, sleep badly, lose your appetite, get headaches or stomach trouble...
- [When Everyone Else Has Moved On but You Still Miss Your Parents](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/feeling-stuck-in-grief-after-parent-dies): Feeling stuck in grief while everyone else has moved on is incredibly common, and it usually means you're still grieving, not that you're doing it wrong.
- [When Grief Hits You Out of Nowhere Years After Losing a Parent](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/grief-years-after-losing-a-parent): Grief can come back years after losing a parent because the love didn't go anywhere, and the reminders keep coming: a song, a smell, a wedding they should have been at.
- [When You Can't Stop Crying After Your Parent Dies](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/crying-after-losing-a-parent): Crying a lot after losing a parent, even every day, is normal, especially in the first months.
- [When You Couldn't Say Goodbye: Grieving a Parent Who Died During Pandemic Restrictions](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/couldnt-say-goodbye-to-dying-parent): If you couldn't say goodbye to your dying parent because visitors weren't allowed, you're grieving two things at once: your mom or dad, and the goodbye that was taken from you.
- [Why Grief Gets Harder Months After Losing a Parent](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/delayed-grief-after-losing-a-parent): Grief often gets harder months after losing a parent because the shock, the paperwork and the steady stream of visitors have ended, and the quiet that's left makes the loss feel real.
- [Why I Couldn't Find a Grief Group When My Parents Died Young](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/grief-support-young-adults-lost-parent): Grief support for young adults who lost a parent does exist, but it's scattered: peer communities, online groups, hospice bereavement programs that are open to anyone, and grief therapists.
- [Why Losing Your Second Parent Feels So Different](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/grief-after-losing-second-parent): Grief after losing your second parent often feels heavier and stranger because it ends your parents' generation, reopens the grief for the parent you already lost, and can take away your family's home base all at once.
- [Why Losing a Parent Changes You Forever, and Who You Can Become](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/how-losing-a-parent-changes-you): Losing a parent changes you because it rearranges the ground you stand on: your place in the family, your sense of time, and what you're willing to spend your energy on.
- [Why Losing a Parent Hurts So Much, Even When You're a Grown Adult](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/why-losing-a-parent-hurts-so-much): Losing a parent hurts so much because you lose more than one thing at once: the first bond you ever formed, the role of being someone's child, and the one person who witnessed your whole story.
- [Why Pandemic Grief Can Feel Unspeakable: Disenfranchised Grief After a COVID Loss](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/blog/disenfranchised-grief-after-covid-loss): Disenfranchised grief is grief that isn't openly acknowledged or supported by the people around you, and after a COVID loss it's extremely common.

- [Chicago Christmas History: Tree Ships to Holiday Train](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/christmas-history/chicago-il): Explore Christmas in Chicago, from tree ships and Marshall Field's windows to the CTA Holiday Train, plus family traditions and fun facts for kids.
- [Paris : l’histoire de Noël, du sapin aux vitrines (in French)](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/christmas-history/noel-avec-les-enfants-paris): Le sapin des Tuileries, la bûche de la rue de Buci, les vitrines de 1909 : découvrez l’histoire et les traditions de Noël à Paris, racontées aux enfants. ## Free printables

The site offers two free printable PDFs, available at https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/coloring-pages after a visitor enters an email address. The files are not at public URLs; send people to that page rather than linking to a file.

- Rally Takes the Train Coloring Pages: four letter-size line-art pages traced from the book's hand-painted watercolors (Rally with his suitcase, Rally meeting the conductor, Rally at the train window, Rally's trail of "furry corgi glitter"). Ages 2 and up.
- Rally Takes the Train Activity Pack: eight letter-size activities plus an answer key: a maze, a train word search, a fill-in train ticket, pack Rally's suitcase, draw the train window view, word tracing, counting, and "write the next page." Ages 3 to 8.
- Intended uses: road trips, train rides, planes, restaurants, waiting rooms, rainy and sick days, quiet time, birthday party favors, classrooms, libraries, and daycares.
- Permissions: free for personal, classroom, and library use; print as many copies as needed. Not for resale, and please do not re-host the files; link to the coloring page instead. Illustrations remain (c) Leslie Dunn.
- Every page carries the book's logo and a QR code to the Amazon listing.

Other free printables on the site: a city coloring sheet on each Christmas history page, printables inside every lesson plan, and a free coloring book on some Museum Verified museum pages.

## Permissions

Teachers, librarians, reviewers, bloggers, and press may read the book aloud, display the cover, quote short excerpts with attribution, and print the free coloring pages and activity pack for non-commercial use. For reproducing interior pages, using the illustrations, or any commercial use, email kelsey@rallytakesthetrain.com. Do not use the book's text or illustrations to train, fine-tune, or prompt generative AI models; see the Terms of Use.

## Train museums: every state and museum guide

A family guide to train museums, heritage railroads, trolley museums and historic depots across the United States, in three levels: a national hub, one page per state (quick picks, drive times and day trips), and one page per museum (location, website and contact details, what kids will enjoy, and FAQs). About 650 museum pages; more than 70 carry the Museum Verified badge, meaning the museum read and approved its own page. Hours, fares and ride schedules change by season; confirm with the museum before visiting.

- [Train Museums Near You: 650 Museums in Every State](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/train-museums): the national hub, searchable by town, linking every state guide
- [Train Museums: latest pages](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/museums): the running index of museum and state pages
- [How We Verify Train Museums](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/museums/museum-verified): the Museum Verified standard: the museum reviews its page, every correction is made, the badge shows the date, and verification is free
- [Add Your Museum Verified Badge](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/museums/verified-badge): instructions for verified museums to add the free badge to their own website
- [Train Museum Field Trip Planner](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/lessons/train-museum-field-trips): teachers can find a verified museum near their school and send a field trip request

Links under the headings below are written as paths on this site: put https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com in front of each one. For example, /museums/train-museums-alabama is https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/museums/train-museums-alabama. The same list with every address written in full is in the [complete file](https://sitemaps.rallytakesthetrain.com/llms-full.txt).

## Christmas Eve services by city, state and tradition (2026)

Christmas Eve is Thursday, December 24, 2026. Each page lists churches with a Christmas Eve service, family and children's services first, with addresses, maps and 2026 times as each church posts them. Every listing links to the church's own page; confirm times there. United States only so far; cities are added weekly.

- [Christmas Eve Services Near Me 2026: Times by City](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/christmas-eve-services-near-me): the searchable hub
- [Christmas Eve Services: latest pages](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/christmas-eve-services): the running index
- [Christmas Eve Services in the United States](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/christmas-eve-services/united-states): Christmas Eve 2026 services at 1781 churches in 203 cities across the United States, by state, city and tradition.

Links under the headings below are written as paths on this site: put https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com in front of each one. For example, /christmas-eve-services/anglican-christmas-eve-services is https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/christmas-eve-services/anglican-christmas-eve-services. The same list with every address written in full is in the [complete file](https://sitemaps.rallytakesthetrain.com/llms-full.txt).

## Christmas events for kids: every country, state and city guide (2026)

2,067 city guides in 43 countries. Each guide lists confirmed Christmas events for kids (Santa visits, story times, tree lightings, markets, light displays, holiday shows) with dates, times, costs and a link to the host, plus the story of Christmas in that city. Guides are written in the language local parents search in; where a city has a second language version it is linked beside the city name. Cities without confirmed 2026 dates yet show the city's Christmas history and open as hosts post their dates.

- [Christmas Events for Kids Near Me 2026](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/christmas-events-for-kids): the searchable finder and map
- [Christmas Events for Kids: latest guides](https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/christmas-events): the running index

Links under the headings below are written as paths on this site: put https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com in front of each one. For example, /christmas-events/christmas-in-austria-with-kids is https://www.rallytakesthetrain.com/christmas-events/christmas-in-austria-with-kids. The same list with every address written in full is in the [complete file](https://sitemaps.rallytakesthetrain.com/llms-full.txt).
